<!-- page 1 -->
NOTICE OF PUBLIC HEARING AND AGENDA
NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS
Tuesday, September 12, 2023
ONE OR MORE SUPERVISORS MAY ATTEND VIA VIDEO AND/OR TELEPHONICALLY 
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors will hold a meeting open to the public on Tuesday, September 12, 2023, in the
Supervisors’ Chamber in the Navajo County Governmental Complex, 100 East Code Talkers Drive, Holbrook, Arizona. The
Board may vote to meet in a closed executive session to discuss certain matters and for legal advice on any item as indicated
on the following agenda, pursuant to A.R.S. § 38-431.03(A) (3). 
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors finds that it is in the best interest of the community to conduct this public meeting using
a hybrid method in order to serve the public interest. 
Join Zoom Meeting, click here: https://us06web.zoom.us/j/87692173340?pwd=WDU3WWhlZFcxd1JqN1BzYXI1dUdpZz09 
 
Call In Number: 1-253-215-8782 OR 1-346-248-7799 (You may use either teleconference number)
Meeting ID: 876 9217 3340
Passcode: 271616
Persons with disabilities who need accommodation to attend or participate in the meeting may contact the Clerk of the Board’s
office at (928) 524-4053; at least 48 hours prior to the meeting, so an accommodation can be arranged.
Items on the agenda may be considered out of order at the Chairman’s discretion. A copy of the agenda background material
provided to the Board, except items to be considered in executive session, is available for public inspection at Navajo County
website, www.navajocountyaz.gov
“NOTICE TO PARENTS AND LEGAL GUARDIANS: Parents and legal guardians have the right to consent before Navajo County makes a video
or voice recording of a minor child, pursuant to A.R.S. § 1-602(A)(9). The Navajo County Board of Supervisors regular meetings are recorded and
may be viewed on Navajo County’s website, and social media pages. If you permit your child to attend/participate in a televised Navajo County
Board of Supervisors meeting, a recording will be made, and your child’s picture may be posted on Navajo County’s social media pages. You may
exercise your right not to consent by not allowing your child to attend/participate in the meeting.” 
9:00 a.m. Call To Order: Invocation and Pledge of Allegiance
           
1. CALL TO THE PUBLIC: Individuals may address the Board on any relevant issue for up
to 3 minutes. At the close of the call to the public, Board members may not respond to any
comments but may respond to criticism, ask staff to review a matter or ask that a matter be
placed on a future agenda.
 
 
2. CONSENT AGENDA: These items are considered to be routine or administrative in nature
and will be approved in a single motion. A Board member may request for any item to be
removed from the consent agenda and considered on the regular agenda:
 
 
a. Minutes: August 17, 2023, Special Meeting and August 22, Regular Meeting  
 
b. Warrant & Voucher Report over $1000, August 2023  
 
c. Contracts signed by County Manager pursuant to Board of Supervisors Resolution: Renewal
of Prepaid Standard Maintenance and Support Services through LogRhythm

<!-- page 2 -->
d. Claim Against Navajo County in the amount of $10, 770 (Craig and Nikole Scarpone)   
 
e. Claim against Navajo County in the amount of $2, 756 (Buckskin Artist Community, Inc.)  
 
f. PY23/FY24 One Stop Operator Agreement between Northeastern Arizona Local Workforce
Development Board and Chicanos Por La Causa, Inc. for LWDBs One Stop Operator as
provided for under QIOA Section 121(d), and to describe in general terms the roles and
responsibilities of the parties to the agreement, procedures for modifications of the
Agreement, compliance requirements and other related purposes
 
 
g. Resignation of Sherry Condarco from the LWDB  
 
h. Amendment to Tall Timbers Park Management Agreement  
 
i. Purchase of radio site equipment in the amount of $100,371.83 through White Mountain
Communications for the existing ARPA Public Safety Communications Project
 
 
j. Extension of the Arizona Internet Crimes Against Children Task Force Intergovernmental
Agreement between the Phoenix Police Department and the Navajo County Sheriff's Office 
 
 
k. Lease agreement with Aero Products Component Services Inc. for up to two hangar spaces
located at the Show Low Airport
 
 
l. Appointment of Deborah Kay Myers, David R. Marshall, Karina Lynn Trumbull and Celia
Joan Laughlin as Republican Precinct Committee members for their respective Precinct
 
 
m. Amendment No. 4, extending the contract with Show Low Ford for one additional year and
setting the price and production year for 2024 vehicles
 
 
n. Intergovernmental Facilities Lease Agreement between the Town of Pinetop-Lakeside and
Navajo County to co-locate Court Services
 
 
o. Letter to the United States Forest Service requesting to expedite permitting for Navajo
County Broadband Project 
 
 
3. ADMINISTRATIVE SERVICES:  
 
a. Presentation to Angela Keene on her retirement after 24+ years of service  
Presented By: Eric Scott/LuAnn Garbini/Judge Nielson
 
b. Consideration of award of Contract #B23-06-014 Code Enforcement Facility (Architect) to
JWA Architects, LLC.
 
Presented By: Grant Evans

<!-- page 3 -->
4. BOARD OF SUPERVISORS:  
 
a. Consideration and approval of a proclamation declaring September 13 through September
16, 2023 as "Navajo County Fair Days".
 
Presented By: Supervisor Jason E. Whiting/Michael Sample/Michelle Stock
 
b. Presentation regarding Navajo County Precincts  
Presented By: RaNae Rowles
 
c. Consideration of an application for a Class B Bingo License for American Legion Auxiliary
Heber-Overgaard Unit 86, located at 2068 Lumber Valley Rd, Overgaard, AZ  85933 (APN
206-30-124A).
 
Presented By: Leah Thomas
 
5. BOARD OF EQUALIZATION:  
 
a. 10:00 a.m. Discussion and action regarding Petition for Review of Real Property Valuation
of APN 409-26-010J (Edwards Revocable Trust); Swearing in of Assessor, Appraisers and
Petitioners.
 
Presented By: Cammy Darris/Melissa Buckley
 
b. 10:05 a.m. Discussion and action regarding Petition for Review of Real Property Valuation
of APN 210-36-004B (Charles Corbett); Swearing in of Assessor, Appraisers and Petitioners.
 
Presented By: Cammy Darris/Melissa Buckley
 
c. 10:20 a.m. Discussion and action regarding Petition for Review of Real Property Valuation
of APN 212-07-002E & 212-07-002F (Lowes Hiw, Inc.); Swearing in of Assessor,
Appraisers and Petitioners.
 
Presented By: Cammy Darris/Melissa Buckley
 
6. NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC HEALTH SERVICES DISTRICT: Board of Directors
Session: 
 
 
a. CONSENT AGENDA: The following item(s) are considered to be of a routine or
administrative in nature and will be approved as a group unless a member of the Board
wishes to discuss a particular item: 
 
 
1. Renewal and update of the billing agreement with Medicare  
 
2. Amendment Number 1 to the Intergovernmental Agreement, CTR059191, to continue to
implement strategies to combat opioid overdose
 
 
3. Memorandum of Understanding between Navajo County Public Health Services District and

<!-- page 4 -->
3. Memorandum of Understanding between Navajo County Public Health Services District and
the Re:Center to enhance our partnership by improving outcomes and lives for Navajo
County residents through enhanced reintegration services
 
 
b. REGULAR AGENDA:  
 
1. Consideration of a proclamation for National Preparedness Month, September 2023.  
Presented By: Catrina Jenkins
 
2. Presentation regarding the 2023 Wildfire Season  
Presented By: Catrina Jenkins
 
7. PUBLIC WORKS:  
 
a. Consideration of the purchase of One International CV515 SFA Truck with Mechanic
Service body style from RWC Group in the amount of $209,886.82.
 
Presented By: Madhav Mundle
 
b. NAVAJO COUNTY FLOOD CONTROL DISTRICT: Board of Directors Session:  
 
1. CONSENT AGENDA: The following item(s) are considered to be of a routine or
administrative in nature and will be approved as a group unless a member of the Board
wishes to discuss a particular item: 
 
 
a. Contract for Professional Services between John Osgood and Navajo County Flood Control
District
 
 
b. Floodplain Variance Application for the existing septic system within the designated FEMA
Floodway at APN 103-24-011
 
 
8. COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE: Report from County Manager, County Attorney and
Board members.
 
 
 
9. ADJOURN  
 
Dated: September 8, 2023 NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
Posted: 12:45 pm By: MWB                          
Melissa W. Buckley - Clerk of the Board

Department: Administrative Services Division: Risk Management
Motion before the Board:
Claim Against Navajo County in the amount of $10, 770 (Craig and Nikole Scarpone)  
Background:
Navajo County employee hit a power pole in front of residence, tearing electrical lines and an
electrical box from the home. Home lost power and an electrician made a temporary repair.
NEC requires a permit, so the electrician ran a mobile solar power unit to home in order to
power home until work was completed. New electrical panel and wiring installed. Fees were
paid for a mobile power unit to power home temporarily. Guest was renting the home at the
time, so the guest was refunded. Total amount paid was $10, 770

Custom
Panel was pulled off the wall by cause of someone hitting the power pole it was
connected to.  Wire was damaged feeding the sub panel from the main panel and
needs to be replaced.  Panel also needs to be replaced with a new riser.  Grounding was
not up to todays code standards and needs updating.
Work Completed
Pull all permits and pass all inspections. 
Replace main meter with a 200-amp 20 space overhead panel.  Install new 13-foot riser
with 4 O/ aluminum wire with a new weather head.  Install point of attachment for
power company to their specs.  Cut a hole through the roof for new riser and install a
boot on riser for weather resistance. 
 Install a #4 bare copper wire with no breaks in the connection to the gas line.  Install a
#4 bare copper wire with no breaks in the connection to the water.  Install a #4 bare
copper wire to a ground rod with no breaks in the connection.  
Replace feeder wire that was at the main panel going to the inside sub panel.  Use 1 O/
SER cable to go from the outside new main panel on a 100-amp breaker to the panel on
the inside.  Since the damaged wire needing replaced was a 3 wire, install a 4-wire
system to be up to today's code standards.  Separate all grounds and neutrals in the sub
panel. 
2-year warranty

Board of Supervisors Regular 2. h.        
Meeting Date: 09/12/2023  
Title: Amendment to Tall Timbers Park Management Agreement
Submitted For: Bryan Layton, County Manager 
Submitted By: Melissa Buckley, Clerk of the Board of Supervisors
Department: Administrative Services
Motion before the Board:
Amendment to Tall Timbers Park Management Agreement
Background:
On June 5, 2007, the County entered into a cooperative agreement with the Heber-Overgaard
Chamber of Commerce for the development, maintenance, and day-to-day administration of the
Tall Timbers Park in the community of Heber-Overgaard in the unincorporated area of Navajo
County.  As part of this cooperative agreement, the County agreed to pay an annual sum of
$8,000 to assist the Chamber in the performance of its obligations. 
The County’s annual contribution to support the Chamber’s administration and care for the park
has not increased since 2007.  To account for inflation and other typical increases to the cost of
doing business, the County proposes to recalculate its annual contribution by an inflationary rate
and increase its annual contribution to $12,000 beginning in the current calendar year 2023. 
Attachments
Tall Timbers Park Agreement 
Amendment Tall Timbers Park 
Form Review
Form Started By: Melissa Buckley Started On: 09/05/2023 11:33 AM
Final Approval Date: 09/05/2023

<!-- page 453 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
AMENDMENT TO TALL TIMBERS PARK 
MANAGEMENT AGREEMENT 
WHEREAS the Heber-Overgaard Chamber of Commerce (“Chamber”) and Navajo County (“County”) are 
parties to a certain Management Agreement for Tall Timbers Park dated June, 2007; and 
WHEREAS, it is the desire of the County and Chamber to amend paragraph 3(a) of said Agreement to 
increase the annual County funding provided to the Chamber for the maintenance and upkeep of the 
park from $8,000 annually to $12,000 annually; and 
WHEREAS, the increased funding is necessary to reflect the additional costs of maintaining the park 
since the original was signed by the parties; 
NOW THEREFORE, 
Paragraph 3(a) of the Agreement is hereby amended to change the County’s annual contribution 
from $8,000 per year to $12,000 per year commencing in calendar year 2023. 
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, the duly authorized representatives of the parties have executed this 
Agreement.

Board of Supervisors Regular 2. j.        
Meeting Date: 09/12/2023  
Title: Arizona Internet Crimes Against Children Task Force
Submitted For: David Clouse, Sheriff 
Submitted By: Kimberley Willis, Sheriff's Office Finance Manager
Department: Sheriff 
Motion before the Board:
Extension of the Arizona Internet Crimes Against Children Task Force Intergovernmental
Agreement between the Phoenix Police Department and the Navajo County Sheriff's Office 
Background:
The current Arizona Internet Crimes Against Children Task Force intergovernmental agreement
between the Phoenix Police Department and the Navajo County Sheriff's Office is set to expire
in October 2023. The Phoenix Police Department is the primary grantee of the US DOJ Office
of Juvenile Justice and Delinquency Prevention grant and, when funds are available, can
provide financial assistance to the Office for the detection and investigation of sexual
exploitation of minors, sexual assaults, and abuse of children, facilitated by technology. The
NCSO Criminal Investigations until will work with a multi-agency task force to respond to the
ICAC offenses. 
The new IGA becomes effective upon the date of the last signature and will remain in effect for
five (5) years, and will supersede any pre-existing agreements.

Title: Space Permit with Aero Products Components Services Inc.
Submitted For: David Clouse, Sheriff 
Submitted By: Kimberley Willis, Sheriff's Office Finance Manager
Department: Sheriff 
Motion before the Board:
Lease agreement with Aero Products Component Services Inc. for up to two hangar spaces
located at the Show Low Airport
Background:
The Navajo County Sheriff's Office has secured up to two (2) hangar spaces located at the
Show Low Airport, by Lessor Aero Products Component Services, Inc. Each hangar space rents
for $400 each per month. The Navajo County Sheriff's Office's Cessna 182 will be housed at the
new space upon approval of the lease. The effective date of permit is September 15th, 2023 or
the date of occupancy. The current hangar also charges a $400 monthly rental fee, so there will
be no increase in price per space.  The Sheriff's Office does not utilize it's general fund for
expenses related to the Cessna 182 beyond fuel costs. Hangar dues are currently paid out of the
NCSO's ARRF allotment and will soon be paid out of new grant money causing no impact on
the Office's budget.

Background:
Appointment valid through Oct. 1, 2024.
16-821. County committee; vacancy in office of precinct committeeman
B. The board of supervisors upon the recommendation of the county chairman, or the recommendation of a
committee designated in the bylaws of the county committee for that purpose, shall determine when a vacancy
exists in the office of precinct committeeman. If a vacancy exists, the vacancy shall be filled by the board of
supervisors from a list of names submitted by the county chairman of the appropriate political party. Only a
precinct committeeman elected at the primary election prior to the date of a state or county committee organizing
meeting shall be permitted to vote at such meeting. The criteria used to establish when a vacancy exists in the office
of precinct committeeman shall be as established in section 38-291.
 
 16-822. Precinct committeemen; eligibility; vacancy; duties; term
Any member of a recognized political party who is a registered voter in the precinct is eligible to seek the office of
precinct committeeman of that party in that precinct.F. The term of office of a precinct committeeman is two years
and begins on October 1 after the primary election at which the precinct committeeman was a candidate and
continues until October 1 after the following primary election at which a precinct committeeman is elected.

Board of Supervisors Regular 2. m.        
Meeting Date: 09/12/2023  
Title: Amendment No. 4, B19-08-019 Annual Contract for Vehicle Replacement Program
Submitted For: Bryan Layton, County Manager 
Submitted By: Cheryl Hollins, Account Specialist
Department: Administrative Services
Motion before the Board:
Amendment No. 4, extending the contract with Show Low Ford for one additional year and
setting the price and production year for 2024 vehicles
Background:
This is an annual contract for vehicle purchases on an "as needed basis" with the option to
renew up to four additional one-year terms, with the concurrence of the vendor. Show Low Ford
is in concurrence and this would be the fourth renewal.  This contract allows for minor additions
and/or deletions and documented price adjustments.  The specifications for the 2024 models
remain the same as prior year models.  Show Low Ford  has a price increase for the 2024
models, which is a result of the increases from the manufacturer. There are a few 2024 models
that currently do not have pricing available and will be added to the contract at a later date.
Staff recommends that the contract be extended one additional year from September 12, 2023
through September 11, 2023.

<!-- page 482 -->
Board of Supervisors Regular 2. n.        
Meeting Date: 09/12/2023  
Title: Partnership Lease with Pinetop-Lakeside to co-locate Court Services
Submitted By: Melissa Buckley, Clerk of the Board of Supervisors
Department: Administrative Services
Motion before the Board:
Intergovernmental Facilities Lease Agreement between the Town of Pinetop-Lakeside and
Navajo County to co-locate Court Services
Background:
The County co-locates Justice Court services with city/town Municipal Courts to more
efficiently deliver court services.  Co-location allows both the County and municipality to save
taxpayer money by sharing resources and building space. 
The Justice Court in Pinetop-Lakeside operates in a building owned and operated by the Town
of Pinetop-Lakeside.  County court business accounts for approximately 75% of the work
performed at the building with Town court business accounting for the other 25%.  This
agreement provides for the County to share in the cost of maintenance and utilities of the
building on a 75/25 basis.  Based on current costs, the County’s share of maintenance and
utilities is $18,000.  The agreement is for 5 years and may be terminated at any time with 30
days notice.
Attachments
IGA Pinetop-Lakeside Court Services 
Form Review
Form Started By: Melissa Buckley Started On: 09/05/2023 02:56 PM
Final Approval Date: 09/05/2023

<!-- page 483 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
 
INTERGOVERNMENTAL FACILITIES LEASE AGREEMENT 
BETWEEN 
THE TOWN OF PINETOP-LAKESIDE AND 
NAVAJO COUNTY, ARIZONA FOR JUSTICE COURT FACILITIES 
 
 
 WHEREAS, the Town of Pinetop-Lakeside, Arizona, a municipal 
corporation (Hereinafter “ Town”), and Navajo Count y, A rizona (Hereinafter 
“County”) and jointly known as the “parties” , desire to enter  into an 
Intergovernmental Fac ilities Lease Agreement  (“Lease”) for Navajo County's 
lease of the Town’s building located at 1630 E. White Mountain Boulevard , 
Pinetop, Arizona 85935; and 
 
 WHEREAS, the Lease allows for more efficient operations between Town 
of Pinetop-Lakeside and Navajo County; and 
 
 WHEREAS, the Lease will allow for more efficient use of space and 
resources for  the Pinetop -Lakeside Justice Court,  thus saving  money for t he 
taxpayers of Navajo County and the Town of Pinetop-Lakeside.  
 
 NOW THEREFORE, the parties agree as follows: 
 
 THE TOWN OF PINETOP-LAKESIDE, ARIZONA , hereby leases the 
Premises described below, for the Term and on the t erms and conditions set 
forth in this Agreement, to NAVAJO COUNTY, ARIZONA (“County”).   
 
SECTION 1 SUMMARY OF BASIC TERMS 
1.1 The Premises.  The Leased Premises (“Premises”) shall consist of  
Units A3 and A4 located at 1630 East White Mountain Boulevard, Pin etop, 
Arizona, 85935.  
1.2 The Term .  The Term of this Lease agreement is Five years (5), 
beginning on  July 1, 2 023 and ending on June 30, 202 8. Upon mutual 
agreement of the parties, the Term may be extended for additional five (5) 
year terms.     
1.3 Rent, Operations and Utilities Costs.  As payment for rent, Navajo County 
agrees to share in the cost of routine maintenance and uti lities as set forth in 
paragraph 4.1. 
1.4 Description of County’s Operations .  Navajo County will  utilize the space 
of the Premises to carry out the functions of govern mental operations for Navajo 
County. It is anticipated that Navajo County wil l operate the Pinetop -Lakeside 
Justice Court  at the Premises . However, the Town understands that  Navajo

<!-- page 484 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
County may utilize the Premises for other Government operations not specifically 
listed in this section. County agrees to co nsult with Town and keep Town 
apprised of active and contemplated uses and improvements. 
1.5 Normal Business Hours. 8:00 a.m. to 5:00 p.m., Monday through Friday.   
1.6 County’s Noti ce Address .  Navajo Count y, P.O. Box 66 8, Holbrook, AZ 
86025.  Attention:  County Manager. 
1.7 Town’s Notice Address .  Town of Pinetop-Lakeside, 325 W. White 
Mountain Blvd., Lakeside, Arizona 85929, Attention: Town Manager. 
1.8 Renewal Period.  County and Town may agree to a renew al of this Lease 
for additional five-year periods as agreed upon  in w riting between the parties  
following the Expiration Date as indicated in paragraph 1.2.   In the absence of a  
renewal between the  County and Town upon the expi ration of this Lease 
agreement and in the event the County holds over as a tenant, the Lease 
agreement shal l continue on a month -to-month basis until such time as either 
party gives (30) days advance notice of intent to terminate the Lease. 
1.9      Prior Agreements.  The parties hereto agree that this Lease Agreement  
supersedes all prior agreements of the parties, including that certain Agreement  
between the same parties for the same Premises executed in May of 2018, and  
the same is hereby terminated. 
 
SECTION 2 DELIVERY AND CONDITION 
2.1 Condition.  County agrees to accept the Premises, and the Town 
agrees to maintain the Premises in good condition. 
SECTION 3 USE OF PREMISES 
3.1 Permitted Uses.  County may use and occupy t he Premises for t he 
purposes set forth in Section 1.4.   
3.2 Access to Premises .  Access to the Premises by the County or the 
Town, and their respec tive  employees, agents , and invitees, shall be 
granted as needed through access cards that will be provided by either the 
Town or the County depending on the area of the building that access  is 
required. The County and the Town agree to immediately notify each other 
of any employee that should no longer be granted access so access can 
be denied for the safety of the public and all employees in the Premises.   
3.3 Insurance Restrictions .  County s hall not perform any act which 
would cause the cancellation of any insurance po licies related to the 
Premises.

<!-- page 485 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
3.4 Improvements.  County shall  consult and receive written permission 
on any improvements to the Court Building after occupation.   
3.5 Compliance with Law .  Without in any manner limiting any other 
provision of this Lease, County hereby represents and warrants and agrees 
for the period of its obligations under this Lease that it shall use and 
maintain the Premises in compliance with all applicable laws. 
SECTION 4 OPERATIONS & UTILITIES COSTS 
4.1 Operation and Utilities Costs.  Navajo County agrees to share in the cost 
of operation and utilities  on a 75/25  basis between the Town (25%) and th e 
County (75%).  Based on current expendi tures, the total cost of routine 
operations and utilities is not expected to ex ceed Twenty-four Thousand Dollars 
($24,000) in Fis cal Year 2022 -2023.  The County agrees to pay the Town the 
amount of Eighteen Thousand Dollars ($18,000) for Fiscal Year 2022-2023. 
The Operation and Utilities costs of the Court building will be reviewed annually 
and discussed between the parties.  Agreed upon increases to the Operation and 
Utilities costs of the Court Building will continue to be shared on a 75/25 basis 
between the Town ( 25%) and the County ( 75%).  The County will pay the Town 
its share of Operation and Utilities costs on or before April 1 of each year during 
the term of this lease.   
SECTION 5 INSURANCE AND INDEMNITY 
5.1 Insurance Pol icies.  The Town will maintain property damage and 
general liability insurance cover ing the Lea sed Premises in the amount 
equal to at least one million dollars ($1,000, 000.) per occur rence.  The 
County shall maintain appropriate general liability and “errors and 
omissions” coverage in the amount equal to at least one million dollars 
($1,000,000.00) per occurrence for the acts and omissions of County or 
Justice Court employees while on the Leased premises. In addition, the 
County shall be solely responsible for providing insurance against loss, 
theft, or damage to personal property owned by the County upon the 
Leased Premises.    
5.2 Indemnity.  County agrees to and shall indemnify, defend and hold 
Town and its off icers, officials,  employees and age nts harmless from and 
against all liability, loss, damage, costs or expenses (including attor ney 
fees and court costs) arising from or as a r esult of the death, bodily injury, 
personal injury, or property damage of any kind or description which may 
directly or indirectly arise out of County's operation of the Leased Premises 
and which to the ex tent directly or indire ctly c aused by any acts or 
omissions of County or its  officers agents or employees.  Town agrees to 
and shall indemnify, defend and hold County and its off icers, officials,  
employees and age nts harmless from and a gainst all liabilit y, loss,

<!-- page 486 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
damage, costs or expenses (includ ing a ttorney fees and court costs) 
arising from  or as a result of the death, bodily injury, personal injury, or 
property damage of any kind or description to the extent  directly or 
indirectly arise out of Town's activities upon the leased premises and which 
may be directly or indirectly caused by any acts or omissions of Town or its 
agents or employees. 5.3 If a claim or claims by th ird parties become 
subject to th e indemnity provision s of this Section 5 , the parties to  this 
Lease shall expeditiously meet to discuss a common and mutual defense, 
including possible proportionate liability and payment of possible litigation 
expenses and damages.  The obligations under this Section 5 shall survive 
termination of this Lease. 
5.2 TTERMINATION RIGHTS OF THE PARTIES 
This Lease may only be cancelled after the initial Term by either party upon One 
Hundred and Eighty (30) days written notice to the other party. 
 
SECTION 6 MAINTENANCE 
6.1 By County.  County shall not be r esponsible for the repairs  or 
replacement to significant structural defects, HVAC system failures, or roof 
replacements.  County shall not be respon sible to maintain, replace, clean 
or repair parking lots, sidewalks, landscaping, o r make appropriate repair s 
to roof or walls, painting and repairs of exterior walls.    
SECTION 7 TOWN RIGHT OF ENTRY 
Town shall have access to the Premises for purposes of i nspection and 
performing Town’s obligati ons and exercising its rights under thi s Lease  upon 
reasonable notice to County. 
SECTION 8   SIGNAGE 
Town and County shall wor k tog ether to provide signage to the Premises that 
complies with the Town’s sign code.  
SECTION 9   COUNTY ALTERATIONS 
9.1 County Alterations.  After orig inally taking possession of the 
Premises, County may make changes, additions, and improvements in the 
Premises, at its own expense.  Such changes, additions or improvements 
shall be in accordance with ap plicable l aw, be made only with the prior 
written con sent of Town, which shall not unreasonably be withheld , be 
constructed in  good workmanlike mann er and o f a quality th at eq uals or 
exceeds the then current standard for the Premises.  The improvements 
shall be cons tructed by County and/or licensed con tractors, who shall 
furnish to Town copies of applicable performance and paymen t bonds.

<!-- page 487 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
The improvements shall be constructed at a reasonable time, based on the 
nature of the work performed at the Premises. 
9.2 County Installations.  County may install o n the  Premises 
reasonable and necessary  fixtures and personal property in a proper 
manner, provided that n o instal lation shall i nterfere with or damage the 
mechanical or electrical systems or the structure of the P remises.  Town 
may require that any work that m ay aff ect structural elements or 
mechanical, electrical, heating, air condition ing, plumbing or other  systems 
be performed by County or by a contractor designated by Town. 
SECTION 10  ASSIGNMENT AND SUBLETTING 
10.1 Consent Re quired.  County shall not assign its interest u nder t his 
Lease or sublet all or any part of the Premises without Town's prior written 
consent, which consent will not unreasonably be withheld.  Any assignment 
of the Lease must meet the Town’s requirements f or a secur e Building.  
The Town may require reasonable identification, financial or other pertinent 
information reasonably req uired to determine the  qualifications of th e 
proposed assignee and/or sub-lessee. 
10.2 Encumbrance Prohibited .  County shall not at any  time pled ge, 
hypothecate, mortgage or otherwise encumber its interest under the Lease 
as security for the payment of a debt o r the performance of a  contract.  
County shall not permit its interest under this Lease to be transferred by 
operation of law.  An y purporte d assignment or Lease made without 
Town's consent shall be void. 
10.3 Continued Responsibility .  County shall remain full y liable for 
performance of the entire  Term of this Lease, notwithstanding its 
assignment or sublease. 
SECTION 11  QUIET ENJOYMENT 
If County observes and performs the terms, covenants and conditions contained 
in this Lease, County shall peaceably and quietly hold and enjoy the Premises for 
the Term, and all renewals thereof, without hindrance or interruption by Town, or 
any other person lawfull y claiming by, through or under Town unless otherwise 
permitted by the terms of this Lease.  County acknowledges that the exer cise by 
the Town of any of the rights conferred on Town under this Lease and the entry 
upon the Premises for or in connection with  such purposes shall not be deemed 
to be a cons tructive or actual eviction of the County and shall not be considered 
to be a breach of Town's covenant of quiet enjoyment. 
SECTION 12   SURRENDER AND HOLDOVER 
12.1 Surrender.  Upon the expiration or termination of this Lease  or of 
County’s right to possession, County shall surrender the Premises in a

<!-- page 488 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
clean undamaged condition and shall remove all o f County’s equipment, 
fixtures and pro perty and repair all damage caused by the removal.  
County shall not remove permanent improv ements that were provided by 
Town at the commencement of this Lease and shall not remove permanent 
improvements later installed by County unless requested in writing to do so 
by Town. 
12.2 Holdover.  If County holds over without Town’s consent, County 
shall, at Town’s election, be a Tenant from month-to-month.  In that event 
Navajo County shall continue to share in the cost of operation and utilities 
on a 75/25 ba sis between the Town (25%) and the County (75% ). All the 
terms of this Lease will remain in effect during the month-to-month tenancy.  
A month-to-month tenancy may be termina ted by either party as of the first 
day of a calendar month upon at least thirty (30) days prior notice. 
SECTION 13  BREACH, DEFAULT, AND REMEDIES 
13.1 Default.  The following shall constitute “Events of Default”: 
(a) County’s failure to pay any amount due under  this Lease within 
twenty (20) days after notice of nonpayment; or 
(b) County’s failure to perform a ny other obligation under this  Lease 
within fifteen days after noti ce of nonperformance; provided, however, that if the 
breach is of such a nature that it canno t be cured within fifteen days, no Event of 
Default shall be deemed to have occurred by reason o f the breach if cure is 
commenced promptly a nd dilig ently pursued t o completion within a period not 
longer than ninety days; and provided further, that in the e vent of a breach 
involving an imminent threat to health or sa fety, Town may in its notice of bre ach 
reduce the period for cure  to such short er period as may be rea sonable under 
the circumstances. 
13.2 Remedies.  Upon the occurrence of an Event of Default, Town, at 
any time thereafter without further notice or demand may exercise any one 
or more of the following remedies concurrently or in succession: 
(a) Terminate County’s r ight to possession of the Premises by legal 
process or otherwise, with or without terminating this Lease, and retake exclusive 
possession of the Premises. 
(b) From time to time relet all or por tions of the Premises, using 
reasonable efforts to mitigate Town’s damages.  In connection with any reletting, 
Town may relet for a period extending beyond the term of this Lease and m ay 
make alterations or improvements t o the Premises without releasing County of 
any liability.  Upon a reletting of all or substantially all of the Premises, Town shall 
be entitled to recover all of its then prospective damages for the balance of the 
Lease Term measured by the difference betw een amounts payable under this 
Lease and the anticipated net proceeds of reletting.

<!-- page 489 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
(c) From time to time recover damages arising from County’s breach of 
the Lease, regardless of whether the Lease has been terminated. 
(d) Recover all costs, expenses and attorney s’ fees incurred by Town 
in connect ion with enforcing this Lease,  recovering po ssession, reletting the  
Premises or collecting amounts owed, including, without limitation, costs of 
alterations, brokerage commissions, and  other costs incurred in connection w ith 
any reletting. 
(e) Pursue other remedies available at law or in equity. 
SECTION 14  NOTICES 
Any notice from one p arty to the other shall be in writing and shall be deemed 
duly served:  (a) if delivered pers onally to a responsible employee of County, 
mailed by registe red or certified mail, return recei pt requested, or sent by 
reputable overnight courier (e.g., FedEx, UPS, DHL) addressed to County at the 
address set forth in Section 1  or (b) mailed by register ed or certified mail, re turn 
receipt requested, or sent by re putable overnight courier (e.g., Fe dEx, UPS, 
DHL) to Town at the address set fo rth in S ection 1 or suc h other address as 
Town may designate.  Any notice shall be deemed to have been given when 
mailed, if mailed, and when delivered, if personally delivered.   
SECTION 15  GENERAL 
15.1 Severability.  If any t erm, covenant or condition of this Lease, or the 
application thereof, is to any extent held or rendered invalid, it shall be and 
is hereby deemed to be independ ent of the remainder of the Lease and to 
be severable and div isible therefrom, and its invalidit y, unenforceability or 
illegality shall not affect, impair or invalidate the remainder of the Lease or 
any part thereof. 
15.2 No Waiver .  The waiver by Town of any b reach of any term, 
covenant or condition contained in this Le ase shall not be deemed to be a 
waiver of such term, covenant, or condition or of any subsequent breach of 
the same or of any other term, covenant or condition contained in this 
Lease.  The subseq uent acceptance of any payment owed by the County  
by Town shall not be deemed to be a waiver of  any preceding breach by 
County of any term, covenant, or condition  of this Lease, regardless of 
Town’s knowledge of such preceding breach at the time of acceptan ce of 
such payment .  No term, covenant or condition of this L ease shall be 
deemed to have been  waived by Town unless such waiv er is in writi ng by 
Town. 
15.3 Delay.  If either party is delayed or hindered in or prevented from the 
performance of any term, covenant or act required hereunder by reasons of 
strikes, labor trou bles, inability to procure materi als or services, power 
failure, restrictive go vernmental laws or re gulations, riots, insurrection,

sabotage, rebellion, war, act of God, or other reason whether o f a like 
nature or not t hat i s beyond the control of the part y affected, financial 
inability e xcepted, then the performance of  that term, co venant o r act is 
excused for the period of the delay and the party delayed shall be entitled 
to perform such term, c ovenant or act within th e app ropriate time period 
after the e xpiration of the period of such d elay.  Nothing in this Section, 
however, shall  excuse County from t he prompt payment of any amount 
payable under this Lease. 
15.4 Successors.  All rights and liabiliti es under this Lease exte nd to and 
bind the successors and ass igns of Town and permitted succes sors and 
assigns of County.  No rights, howeve r, shall  inure to the  benefit of any 
transferee of the County unless the transfer has been consented to by the 
Town in writing as provided in Section 12.1.  If there is more tha n one sub-
tenant, they are all bound jointly and severally by the terms, covenants and 
conditions of this Lease. 
15.5 Integration.  This Lease and exhibits hereto attached, set forth all 
the covenants , promises, agreements, conditions and understandings 
between Town and County concerning the Premises and there are no other 
covenants, promises, agreements, conditions or understandings, either oral 
or written, between them.  No alteration, amendment or a ddition to this 
Lease shall be binding upon Town or County unless in writing and signed 
by County and Town. 
15.6 Dispute Resolution .  All dispute s and co ntroversies b etween the 
parties hereto shall be resolved by mediation, arbitration or such other 
alterative dispute resolution to which the parties may agree.  In the event of 
a failure to agree on a di spute resolution forum within th irty (30) days  of 
written notice of  the dispute, either party may seek relief in Navajo County 
Superior Court. 
15.7 Governing Law.  Thi s Lease shall be constru ed in accordance with 
and governed by  the laws of the State of Arizona , with venue in Navajo 
County. 
15.8 Deadlines Enforceable.  Time is of the essence of this Lease and of 
every part hereof. 
15.9 Counterparts.  This Lease may be executed in  counterparts, which 
together shall constitute a single instrument. 
15.10 Mutual Representations and Warranties.  The person(s) sign ing this 
Lease and any  documents an d instruments in connection herewith on

so.  The execution, delivery, and perform ance of this Lease by the

NAVAJO COUNTY  
Board of Supervisors 
 
 
  
Fern Benally • Alberto L. Peshlakai •Jason E. Whiting • Daryl Seymore • Dawnafe Whitesinger

Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
 
 
 
September 7, 2023 
 
 
 
United States Forest Service 
Apache-Sitgreaves National Forests, Supervisor’s Office 
Attn: Robert Lever, Forest Supervisor 
30 S. Chiricahua Dr.  
Springerville, AZ 85938 
Email: SM.FS.ASNF_PAO@usda.gov 
 
Re: Request to expedite permitting for Navajo County Broadband Project 
 
Dear Supervisor Lever, 
 
On behalf of Navajo County, I’d like to say thank you to your staff for taking the time to meet 
with our staff on August 31, 2023. I understand that this meeting to discuss Navajo County’s 
Middle Mile Broadband Project was very productive. We are thankful for your cooperative effort 
to put our project into consideration at your next “small NEPA projects” meeting on September 
13th.  At that meeting, we respectfully ask your consideration to prioritize our project and 
expedite permitting approval in any ways possible. Our project includes 105 miles of open-
access internet fiber critically needed to bridge the digital divide in our rural Arizona County. 
This project has been funded through Coronavirus U.S. Treasury Capital Project Funds along 
with American Rescue Plan ACT funding. The project has been recognized by the Arizona 
Commerce Authority, U.S. Treasury, and Arizona’s Congressional Delegation, including 
Senators Sinema and Kelly, as a critical project needed to bridge the digital divide in rural 
America. This project supports the objective of the Capital Projects Fund in addressing the 
challenges laid bare by the pandemic, especially in rural America and low- and moderate-income 
communities, helping to ensure that all communities have access to the high-quality, modern 
infrastructure needed to thrive, including internet access. (US Treasury Website) 
Treasury Announces Three Additional Capital Projects Fund Awards, Connecting Nearly 
190,000 Homes and Businesses to Affordable, High-Speed Internet | U.S. Department of the 
Treasury. 
 
The public value of Navajo County’s project is undeniable. However, for this project to be 
successful, it needs to be completed expediently and construction needs to begin this calendar 
year. The U.S Treasury Capital Projects fund require that the project be completed by July 2025. 
This can only be accomplished if construction begins soon. Additionally, this middle mile is 
critical infrastructure needed to support the federal BEAD program to further complete 
construction that will bring internet access to all unserved and underserved areas of Arizona.

<!-- page 495 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
I understand and appreciate the monumental responsibility you have in managing and protecting 
our valuable national forest lands and am greatly appreciative of you and your team’s efforts. 
Navajo County does not make this request lightly, but without your support in prioritizing this 
project, tens of thousands of residents and businesses in Navajo County will be left further 
behind in an ever-expanding digital world.   Delays in permitting could jeopardize this once in a 
lifetime opportunity to bridge this digital divide. Additionally, we submit that the approvals for 
this project should be relatively easy to expedite as the project involves only a very small trench 
not much more than a groove needed to install one and one quarter inch conduits within the 
existing highway rights of way along US 40, SR 77, and  SR 260. 
 
In discussions with your staff, we have been informed that our project will qualify for a NEPA 
Categorical Exclusion and the level of prioritization could be considered at your September 13th 
meeting. We respectfully request that you assign the highest priority to this project so that it may 
be cleared through the process and approved for construction to begin later this fall.  
 
Thank you for taking the time to consider our request. Please do not hesitate to reach out to our 
team for further discussions on solutions. We are certainly willing to partner to help facilitate a 
successful outcome for the people we serve. We look forward to working with you. For technical 
and strategic processing questions, please reach out directly to either: Glenn Kephart at 480-784-
7312 Glenn.Kephart@Navajocountyaz.gov or Grant Evans at Grant.Evans@Navajocountyaz.gov 
 
 
Sincerely,  
 
Alberto Peshlakai, Chairman 
Navajo County Board of Supervisors

Title: Contract #B23-06-014 Code Enforcement Facility (Architect)
Submitted For: Grant Evans, Capital Projects Manager 
Submitted By: Jeanine Carruthers, Deputy Director Administration
Department: Administrative Services
Presented By: Grant Evans
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of award of Contract #B23-06-014 Code Enforcement Facility (Architect) to
JWA Architects, LLC.
Background:
This contract will provide architectural and engineering design services for a new Code
Enforcement Facility on County-owned property at 1121 W. Pinedale Road in Taylor, AZ.  This
new facility will provide for the consolidating of the Medical Examiner, Animal Control and
Environmental Health teams into one new facility.  Also included in this new building is a
Sheriff Substation.
Sealed proposals were received by the Board of Supervisors at the Navajo County Government
Center, Holbrook, Arizona until 3:00 p.m. M.S.T., July 26, 2023. No proposals were accepted
after 3:00 p.m.  The proposals were publicly opened and read aloud at 3:15 p.m. The proposals
were evaluated per the criteria set forth in the request for qualifications. 
Recommendation: 
The Evaluation Committee is recommending award of Contract #B23-06-014 Code
Enforcement Facility (Architect) to JWA Architects, LLC.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 24 & 25
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: $311,900
Fiscal Impact:
The funding for this project includes $1,510,490 from ARPA funds and $2,239,510 from
general funds.  A portion of the general funds will be used for this design phase.

and JWA Architects, LLC (“Consultant”). 
 
WHEREAS, County requires certain professional services for Code Enforcement 
Facility, Contract No. B23-06-014 (“Project”); and 
 
WHEREAS, Consultant represents that it has the necessary expertise to provide such 
services in accordance with the terms of this Agreement. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, the parties agree as follows: 
 
1. Services.  Consultant shall perform the work described in the Scope of Services 
Revised August 31, 2023,  and those additional items set forth in the Consultant’s 
schedule and fee proposal Revised August 31, 2023, in a competent and professional 
manner to the satisfaction of County.  Th e Scope of Services and the Consultant’s 
schedule and fee proposal are attached he reto and by this reference incorporated 
herein. 
 
2. Contract Documents.
 The Contract Documents comprise this Contract, Request for 
Qualifications No. B23-06-014, Proposal Submittal dated July 26, 2023, Consultants 
Scope of Services and Fee Proposal Revised August 31, 2023, and addenda thereto, if 
any. If any incorporated term is inconsistent  with the Contract, this Contract shall 
control. 
 
3. Consultant’s Expertise.  Consultant warrants that it has the ability, authority, 
capacity and professional expertise to perfo rm this Agreement.  Consultant shall

Re:   Navajo County Taylor Code Enforcement Building Fee Revised Proposal 
  Architectural and Engineering Services 
 
Dear Jeanine, 
JWA Architects is pleased to offer architectural and engineering services in support of the Navajo County Taylor 
Code Enforcement Facility project . This proposal is based on our Response to the County’s Request for  
Qualifications RFQ B23-06-014.  This fee proposal is intended to be made as an attachment to that response and 
attached to the County’s contract for architectural and engineering services. 
 
This fee is based on using JWA’s initial conceptual floor plan as a starting point.  We anticipate changes related to 
the sheriff’s additional program requirements, and routine revisions due to client reviews of the developing 
design.  
 
BASIC SERVICES - SCOPE OF WORK. Basic services will be as generally defined in the AIA Standard Owner Architect 
Agreement.  These will include: 
• Architectural Services. JWA will provide architectural design, construction documents and 
construction administration.  We will provide limited interior design for fixed building elements, 
casework, lab equipment, colors, and finishes. Specifications will be provided in the drawings, with a 
book type Division 0-Bidding Requirements, prepared in coordination with County procurement staff 
and containing applicable federal requirements. 
• Civil Engineering for site grading, hydrology, site fire and domestic water,  and erosion control.  A 
traffic letter, drainage report, ADEQ submittal as needed, rough grading and final grading certification 
are included.  Septic system design is also included. 
• Landscape Design. JWA will provide a landscape plan, showing basic plantings, and the irrigation 
system, to meet Town of Taylor minimum requirements. Extensive landscaping is not anticipated. 
• Structural Engineering, for building foundations and the building structure, including studying options 
for alternative building systems as needed.

<!-- page 513 -->
• Mechanical and Plumbing Engineering. Package or split HVAC units are planned. The engineer will 
provide a final inspection of the system and review O & M manuals.  Commissioning services are not 
anticipated, due to the simplicity of the roof top package unit design.  Commissioning is available as 
an additional service. The contract documents will require that the contractor hire an independent 
test and balance contractor, who will prepare a report to be reviewed by the engineer.  Plumbing 
design will include design requirements and specifications for the fire sprinkler system. 
• Electrical Engineering, including site lighting and power, design for emergency generator,  provision 
for future solar,  building basic lighting and power,  fire alarm design requirements, and rough  in 
requirements for owner’s telecom/data systems.  
 
 
FEE FOR BASIC SERVICES. We will provide the Basic Services identified above for a fixed fee of $240,000.  
The fee will be charged by phase of the work, based on percentage completion of each phase: 
1. Schematic design     15%  $  36,000 
 
2. Design development  25%  $  60,000 
 
3. Construction documents  40%  $  96,000 
 
4. Bidding        5%  $  12,000 
 
 
5. Construction administration  15%  $  36,000 
Construction duration of 40 weeks anticipated, with time for 20 architectural construction phase visits 
included in Basic Services.  A total of six engineering site visits are also included.  Extra visits can be 
provided as Additional Services when needed. 
 
FEE FOR REQUIRED ADDITONAL SERVICES.  We will provide these required additional services for a fixed fee of 
$56,900. 
 
1. Civil Engineering and traffic letter    $  32,000 
2. Civil Septic Design      $    8,000 
3. Topographic and boundary survey    $    9,000 
4. Geotechnical engineering     $    7,900 
 
TOTAL FEE FOR BASIC AND REQUIRED ADDITIONAL SERVICES     $296,900 
 
ADDITIONAL OPTIONAL SERVICES.  Additional services can be provided upon owner request.  Each  additional 
authorization will be via additional proposals, as amendments to the professional services contract.  
 
OWNER PROVIDED ITEMS.  Navajo County will provide: 
• Information on any Owner-provided equipment, existing or planned. 
• Interior design and specifications for furniture 
• Design for telecom/data systems. 
• Design for audio / video systems.  
• Design for fire sprinkler and alarm systems.  JWA will provide Code design requirements, drawings to be 
provided by general contractor’s subcontractor. 
• Fire hydrant flow testing, if not available through the Town of Taylor. 
• Single point of contact. 
• Special structural inspections.  
• Construction material testing.

<!-- page 514 -->
• As built surveying.  This is required for the rough and final grading certificates prepared b y our civil 
engineer.  This is normally provided by the general contractor with his construction surveyor. 
 
REIMBURSABLE EXPENSES.  In addition to the fixed fees for professional services, these services will be provided 
and charged as reimbursable expenses. They will be charged at 1.15 times their direct expense to the Architect:  
a. Travel. We anticipate travel to Navajo County will be required to meet the needs of this project 
during design, and regular construction phase site visits in Taylor.  Time for this travel is included 
in Basic or Additional Services above.   We will charge as reimbursable costs for gas based on trip 
mileage. 
b. Printing and express deliveries.  
We suggest a budget of $15,000 for potential reimbursable expenses. 
 
SCHEDULE.  We can start work immediately upon your authorization.  Our work approach and preliminary 
schedule were presented in our response to the RFQ.  We will review and update this at our kickoff meeting with 
your staff.   
 
ACCEPTANCE.  We will review Navajo County’s standard contract or  prepare a formal standard AIA 
Owner/Architect Agreement for your review.  Please let me know if you have any questions.  Thank you for this 
opportunity to continue working together. 
Sincerely,    
 
 
Tim Whiteside, Partner

Motion before the Board:

2023 as "Navajo County Fair Days".
Background:
This year marks the 92nd Annual Navajo County Fair & Rodeo with the theme of "Country Life
and Exciting Nights at the Navajo County Fair"
 
Entertainment this year includes: the Ram Rodeo, Demolition Derby, Little Buckaroo Rodeo,
Open Class Exhibits and Demonstrations, Jr. Livestock Show and Auction, 4-H Horse Show,
Small Stock Show and Exhibits.  There will also be the Red Dirt Music Festival Concert
featuring Steven Denmakr, Ronnie and the Redwoods and Stateline Band on Wednesday night,
Live Band and Dance featuring Noise Pollution, the AC/DC Tribute Band, on Thursday
following the Demolition Derby, Live Music and Dance featuring Kenny Feidler & the Cowboy
Killers opened by Tim Lightyear on Friday following the Rodeo and Live Band and Dance
featuring Last Train to Juarez Band following the Rodeo.  Plus the carnival, food booths,
vendors, and much, much more.

Navajo County 
Proclamation 
 
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
 
SEPTEMBER 13, 2023 THROUGH SEPTEMBER 16, 2023 
“NAVAJO COUNTY FAIR DAYS” 
 
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Fair is celebrating its 92nd Year in succession; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Fair has been an important part of Navajo County history 
since the 1930s; and 
 
WHEREAS, citizens can display garden and homemade products, artistic pieces of work, 
livestock and their hobbies; and 
 
WHEREAS, this annual event is a wholesome, fun, and educational experience which has 
been long enjoyed by all; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Board of Supervisors and the residents of Navajo County 
value the benefits that the Fair provides to the citizens of Navajo County. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, I, Alberto L. Peshlakai, Chairman of the Navajo County Board of 
Supervisors, do hereby proclaim September 13 through September 16, 2023, as  
 
“NAVAJO COUNTY FAIR DAYS” 
 
in Navajo County and thank the volunteers who are contributing their time and energy to 
making the Fair a success and urge all residents of Navajo County and the Navajo County 
staff to take part in the festivities and join in the celebration of Navajo County’s 92nd Annual 
County Fair. 
 
Done in concurrence with the Navajo County Board of Supervisors Meeting this 12th day of 
September 2023. 
 
_______________________________ 
Alberto Peshlakai, Chairman

<!-- page 520 -->
What is a Precinct?
Is Navajo County in compliance with ARS 16-411?

<!-- page 521 -->
2
Precinct Definition
⚫ In the United States, an electoral precinct or voting district is the 
smallest unit into which electoral districts are divided. A larger 
geographic unit such as a county, township, or city council district is 
typically subdivided into precincts and each address is assigned to a 
specific precinct. Each precinct has a specific polling station where 
its residents go to vote; however, more than one precinct may use 
the same polling station.

<!-- page 522 -->
3
ARS 16-411   
Designation of election precincts and polling places
A. The board of supervisors of each county, on or before October 1 of 
each year preceding the year of a general election, by an order, shall 
establish a convenient number of election precincts in the county and 
define the boundaries of the precincts as follows:
1. The election precinct boundaries shall be established so as to be 
included within election districts prescribed by law for elected officers 
of the state and its political subdivisions, including community college 
district precincts, except those elected officers provided for in titles 30 
and 48.

<!-- page 523 -->
4
Political Subdivision (definition)
ARS 38-431
"Political subdivision" means all political subdivisions of this 
state, including without limitation all counties, cities and towns, 
school districts and special districts.

<!-- page 524 -->
5
Navajo County precinct statistics
⚫ A 2004 survey by the United States Election Assistance Commission 
reported an average precinct size in the United States of 
approximately 1,100 registered voters. Kansas had the smallest 
average precinct size with 437 voters per precinct, while the District 
of Columbia had the largest average size at 2,704 voters per 
precinct.
⚫ Precinct sizes in Navajo County range from 241 to 12,795 voters.
⚫ Average precinct size in Navajo County is 5,464 voters.

<!-- page 525 -->
6
Arizona Counties (2020)     # Precincts/Active voters/Av. Size
⚫ Apache 44/ 51,906 / 1,179
⚫ Cochise 49 / 82,018 / 1,674
⚫ Coconino 71 / 90,716 / 1,278
⚫ Gila 39 / 34,068 / 874
⚫ Graham 22 / 19,851 / 902
⚫ Greenlee 8 / 4,866 / 608

⚫ Maricopa 743 / 2,595,272 / 3,493

⚫ Yuma 44 / 103,273 / 2,347

<!-- page 526 -->
What is the difference 
between a “precinct” and a 
“precinct part”?
7

<!-- page 527 -->
8
Precincts
⚫ Precinct boundaries are used to compute the number of Precinct 
Committeemen (PC) that will represent a group of voters living in an 
area with similar representation and concerns. Each precinct 
automatically receives 1 PC plus additional PCs based on the 
number of party-registered voters.
⚫ Precincts are used to determine community-based polling locations 
where voters share the same ballot style, particularly for local races.
⚫ If necessary, adjacent precincts may be combined at the same 
polling location.

<!-- page 528 -->
9
Precinct Parts
⚫ Precinct parts are a way of tracking different ballot styles within a 
precinct.
⚫ ARS 16-411 explicitly states that elected officers provided for in 
Titles 30 and 48 do not need to be accounted for in creating precinct 
boundaries.  
⚫ These officers relate to special districts, such as fire and flood 
districts.

<!-- page 529 -->
10
Example
⚫ Citizens living in municipal Pinetop-Lakeside might be part of Timber Mesa 
Fire District or Pinetop Fire District.
⚫ If the town of Pinetop-Lakeside were its own precinct (Precinct 12) then the 
precinct parts might be 12.01 and 12.02 to distinguish between the different 
fire districts on precinct ballots
⚫ Municipal Show Low might be Precinct 13 and portions of Precinct 13 that 
include Timber Mesa Fire District might be shown as precinct part 13.01.
⚫ Precinct parts are not guaranteed separate representation when electing PCs 
and might not be considered as “communities of interest” when redistricting 
occurs.

<!-- page 530 -->
What are some political 
subdivisions within Navajo 
County?
11

<!-- page 531 -->
12
Political Subdivisions found on maps
⚫ Navajo Nation Chapters
⚫ Incorporated Municipalities
⚫ School Districts
⚫ Congressional and Legislative Districts (redistrict every 10 years)
⚫ Board of Supervisor and Justice of the Peace Districts (redistrict as 
needed)

<!-- page 532 -->
13
Precinct Boundary Recommendations
⚫ Navajo Nation Judicial Districts
⚫ School Districts (fixed)
⚫ Municipalities (fixed unless annexation occurs)

with fixed school district and municipal boundaries

Motion before the Board:
Renewal and update of the billing agreement with Medicare
Background:
This agreement went dormant. When we reopened our account we realized there were updates
to personnel and approvers that needed to be made. This document makes the changes needed
for our agreement to be current. We request signatures from Chairman Peshlakai on the

Board of Supervisors Regular 6. a. 2.        
Meeting Date: 09/12/2023  
Title: CTR059191 Amendment 1 Senate Bill 1847
Submitted For: Janelle Linn, Health Director 
Submitted By: Amy Stradling, Department Manager
Department: NC Public Health Services District
Motion before the Board:
Amendment Number 1 to the Intergovernmental Agreement, CTR059191, to continue to
implement strategies to combat opioid overdose
Background:
More than five people a day in Arizona die from opioid overdoses. On June 30,2021, Governor
Ducey passed a bill allowing County Health Departments to utilize funding from medical
marijuana sales to address public health issues and communities affected by drug addiction.
This will be done through overdose fatality reviews, Narcan training and distribution and using
the community connection team to link individuals to resources, treatment and care.

Title: MOU NCPHSD & Re:Center

Motion before the Board:
Memorandum of Understanding between Navajo County Public Health Services District and
the Re:Center to enhance our partnership by improving outcomes and lives for Navajo County
residents through enhanced reintegration services
Background:
Navajo County Public Health Services District is formalizing a partnership with the Re:Center
to enhance services surrounding Substance Use Disorder and reintegration services.  This
includes but is not limited to education, case management, evaluating policies, distribution of
life-saving measures,and linking individuals to care.

MEMORANDUM OF UNDERSTANDING 
Between 
Navajo County Public Health Services District (NCPHSD) 
and 
Re:Center 
 
This Memorandum of Understanding (MOU) is between Navajo County Public Health Services 
District (NCPHSD) and Re:Center. 
 
I. PURPOSE & SCOPE 
 
The purpose of this Memorandum of Understanding (MOU) is to clearly identify the roles and 
responsibilities of the said parties related to the collaboration between these entities to stimulate and 
enhance services surrounding Substance Use Disorder and reducing recidivism rates.  This includes, 
but is not limited to education, case management, evaluating practices and policies, distribution of 
life saving resources, and implementation of systematic changes around safety and linking 
individuals to care.  
 
II. BACKGROUND 
 
In November 2020, Prop 207 passed legalizing recreational use Marijuana in Arizona.  Navajo 
County Public Health Services District receives periodic distributions for a percentage of 
recreational marijuana sales tax.  Partnership with the Re:Center Re-Entry Program is a beneficial 
and responsible way to support them as they enhance their services.  The Re:Centers services offer 
aid those working to re-enter and reintegrate into society, helping them learn skills, connect them 
with services, and linkage to treatment.  These services improve the probability of success and 
reduce recidivism rates.  
 
III. HEALTH DISTRICT RESPONSIBILITIES 
• Two annual payments will be made to the Re:Center, in the amount of $10,000 each. 
Disbursements will be made in January and June dependent on funding availability and 
requirements.  The first allocation will be in January 2024. 
• To be evaluated annually to determine availability of funding and impact of program 
• Work with ADHS to report outcomes, partnership, etc.

IV. Other Organizations Responsibilities: 
• Designate a point of contact to work with NCPHSD 
• Sign the MOU with NCPHSD 
• Sign a data sharing agreement with NCPHSD 
• Provide data to NCPHSD on a quarterly basis 
o Demographics of population being served 
o Number of individuals served/assisted 
o Outcomes and impacts (backed by data) 
• Provide a quarterly narrative report summarizing activities the funding is being utilized 
to support to NCPHSD  
 
V. Terms and Conditions: 
 
Both parties understand and agree this Memorandum is contingent on the availability of funds 
and data supporting program success.  Proposals considered by the Legislature, including 
those made by the Governor could potentially reduce or defer these funds at any time.  The 
Memorandum shall terminate immediately and automatically upon the termination of funds 
for any reason.  In addition, either party may terminate this Memorandum upon 30 days with 
prior written notice. 
 
VI. FUNDING 
 
Navajo County Public Health Services District will supply the Re:Center with financial aid to 
help support and enhance their re-entry program services.  Both parties have agreed to a 
$10,000 disbursement every six months, contingent on funding availability.  The 
Memorandum shall terminate immediately and automatically upon the termination or decrease 
of funding for any reason and both parties agree to this. 
 
VII. Effective Date & Signature 
 
This memorandum of Understanding (MOU) shall be effective upon the signature of Navajo 
County Public Health Services District and ________________________ authorized officials.  
It shall be in force from _____________ to _______________________.  All parties indicate 
agreement by their signatures.

Board of Supervisors Regular 6. b. 1.        
Meeting Date: 09/12/2023  
Title: National Preparedness Month 2023 - Take Control in 1, 2, 3!
Submitted For: Catrina Jenkins, EMP Director 
Submitted By: Catrina Jenkins, EMP Director
Department: NC Public Health Services District Division: Emergency Management
Presented By: Catrina Jenkins
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of a proclamation for National Preparedness Month, September 2023.
Background:
Navajo County Emergency Management and Preparedness would ask that the Navajo County
Board of Supervisors recognize and approve the proclamation for National Preparedness Month
2023. The theme for this year's campaign is "Take Control in 1, 2, 3!" and focuses on our older
citizens and how they can prepare for disasters. It is imperative that all of our citizens be ready
to face disasters that could happen at any moment. Navajo County has experience floods,
wildfires, communications and power failures as well as hazardous materials incidents. 
Step 1: make a plan! Plan for your communications, transportation if you need to evacuate,
ensure your important papers are in order.
Step 2: make a kit, with food, water and medications for everyone in your house (including pets)
Step 3: stay aware, know the disasters that could affect your ara, sign up for Ready Navajo
County emergency alerts and follow vetted sources of information for instructions and updates.
By taking 3 steps to be ready for any disaster our citizens will be more resilient should they
ever experience a disaster. 
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends approval.
Attachments
NPM Proclamation 2023

Form Started By: Catrina Jenkins Started On: 08/29/2023 09:55 AM
Final Approval Date: 08/29/2023

<!-- page 649 -->
Navajo County               
Proclamation 
 
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
NATIONAL PREPAREDNESS MONTH 2023 
WHEREAS, Navajo County has experienced severe weather, floods, wildfires, communication outages 
and public health emergencies which can interrupt lives, damage property and infrastructure and 
threaten the safety of Navajo County residents; and 
WHEREAS, the theme “Take Control in 1, 2, 3” which will focus on preparing older adults for 
disasters. While all citizens should take the steps to be prepared for all-hazard events, older adults may 
face greater risks when it comes to threats and hazards in Navajo County especially if they are living 
alone, are low-income or have a disability; and 
WHEREAS, all Navajo County citizens are encouraged to prepare for disasters by 1) developing a family 
communication plan, 2) build an emergency kit, 3) learn the hazards of your community; where you live, 
work and travel and inspire others by your preparedness example; and 
WHEREAS, Navajo County citizens are encouraged to sign up for the Ready Navajo County emergency 
alerting system and follow the three principles of the Ready, Set, Go! Program: be READY year round for 
any emergency by preparing now for what threatens your community, be SET by maintaining awareness 
of significant danger and GO!, evacuate immediately when danger is imminent and life-threatening; and 
WHEREAS,  The Board of Supervisors would like to officially recognize the hard work and dedication of all 
those involved in ensuring the safety of our citizens and their property, businesses and communities, including 
first responders, volunteers, non-governmental agencies and most of all the citizens themselves.  
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT PROCLAIMED, by the Board of Supervisors of Navajo County that 
September of 2023 is designated as “NATIONAL PREPAREDNESS MONTH 2023” in Navajo County. 
 
PASSED, ADOPTED AND APPROVED by the Board of Supervisors on September 12, 2023. 
 
         ____________________________ 
Alberto Peshlakai, Chairman

Board of Supervisors Regular 7. a.        
Meeting Date: 09/12/2023  
Title: Purchase of One International CV515 SFA Service Truck
Submitted For: Madhav Mundle, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Jeanine Carruthers, Deputy Director Administration
Department: Public Works
Presented By: Madhav Mundle
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of the purchase of One International CV515 SFA Truck with Mechanic Service
body style from RWC Group in the amount of $209,886.82.
Background:
This truck would be utilized as a Field Service Truck which would eliminate the need to
transport trucks/equipment from the Heber yard to either Show Low or Holbrook for any needed
repairs.
The State of Arizona has a contract with RWC Group (Contract No. CTR041813) for the
purchase of Medium and Heavy Duty Cabs, Chasis, and Buses and this contract is available
for cooperative use.  RWC does have this truck in stock.
The price under this contract is fair and reasonable.  International has the ability to read
diagnostic codes via On-Demand Connect versus bringing the truck into the shop for
diagnostics.  This saves travel time and money on repairs.
 
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends approval of the purchase of One International CV515 SFA Truck with
Mechanic Service body style from RWC Group as presented.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: FY 2024
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: 209886.82

<!-- page 676 -->
Board of Supervisors Regular 7. b. 1. b.        
Meeting Date: 09/12/2023  
Title: Variance for septic system in FEMA floodway
Submitted For: Bill Bess, County Engineer/Assistant Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Julianna Davis, Hydrologly Technician 
Department: Public Works
Motion before the Board:
Floodplain Variance Application for the existing septic system within the designated FEMA
Floodway at APN 103-24-011
Background:
The Applicant, Della King, has requested her existing septic system be brought up to comply to
current County codes. The existing septic tank and leach field was installed in an existing
FEMA Floodway, north of Winslow, on Assessor Parcel Number 103-24-011, located on the
southwest corner of N. Park Drive and W. McHood Road. The property is located on FEMA
Flood Insurance Rate Map Number 04017C3004F, dated March 2, 2016. The map indicates that
the property is in a FEMA Floodway, approximately 1,600 feet in width. Because of the wide
Floodway width, it is evident that the floodwaters will be flowing at a shallow depth, as well as
low velocities. With these flow characteristics, the septic system and leach field will have a
minimal probability of damage from erosion. The Navajo County Flood Damage Prevention
Ordinance allows for a variance for this type of condition.
